import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import OfficialQuestionPane from "./OfficialQuestionPane";

const question = {
  qid: "q-26",
  qnum: 26,
  stimulus:
    "A recent poll showed that almost half of the city's residents believe that Mayor Walker is guilty of ethics violations.",
  questionStem:
    "Which one of the following, if true, most helps to explain the surprising fact stated above?",
  answerChoices: {
    A: "Almost all of the people who believe that Walker is guilty of ethics violations had thought his performance was poor.",
    B: "There has been an increase in ratings for Walker's political opponents.",
  },
};

function renderPane(questionOverride = question) {
  return render(
    <OfficialQuestionPane
      question={questionOverride}
      displayNumber={26}
      selectedAnswer="A"
      eliminatedAnswers={new Set()}
      isFlagged={false}
      answerRevealed
      correctAnswer="A"
      highlights={[]}
      highlightMode="none"
      onAnswerSelect={vi.fn()}
      onEliminate={vi.fn()}
      onToggleFlag={vi.fn()}
      onTextSelection={vi.fn()}
      onHighlightClick={vi.fn()}
    />,
  );
}

describe("OfficialQuestionPane", () => {
  it("keeps the revealed correct answer readable on the official white surface", () => {
    const { container } = renderPane();

    const root = container.firstElementChild;
    expect(root).toHaveClass("bg-white");
    expect(root).not.toHaveClass("bg-bg-shadow");

    const correctAnswerText = screen.getByRole("button", {
      name: /Almost all of the people who believe that Walker is guilty/i,
    });
    expect(correctAnswerText).toHaveClass("text-green-900");
    expect(correctAnswerText).not.toHaveClass("text-green-50");
  });

  it("renders multi-word speaker labels as separate dialogue turns", () => {
    const { container } = renderPane({
      ...question,
      stimulus:
        "Figorian Wildlife Commission: First speaker text.\n\nFigorian Development Commission: Second speaker text.",
    });

Each section is its own raw string with its own\n * 0-based offsets: the stimulus, the stem, and every answer choice by letter.\n * Offsets from one section never mean anything in another, which is why every\n * overlap test below is scoped to the section first.\n */\nexport type HighlightSection = 'stimulus' | 'stem' | `choice:${string}`;\n\nexport function choiceSection(key: string): HighlightSection {\n  return `choice:${key}`;\n}\n\nexport interface Highlight {\n  id: string;\n  start: number;\n  end: number;\n  text: string;\n  color: HighlightColor;\n  section: HighlightSection;\n}\n\n/**\n * A highlightable block on screen: the element that holds one section's text\n * and the raw string it was rendered from. The source string is what offsets\n * are measured against, so `text` on a stored highlight is always a slice of\n * it rather than whatever the DOM happened to serialise.\n */\nexport interface HighlightRegion {\n  section: HighlightSection;\n  container: HTMLElement;\n  source: string;\n}\n\nexport interface CapturedSelection {\n  section: HighlightSection;\n  start: number;\n  end: number;\n  text: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Clamp the live selection to `container`. Returns null when the selection is\n * collapsed or does not touch the container at all. A selection that starts\n * before the container (on the question number, in the previous block) or\n * runs past its end is cut at the container's edges rather than rejected,\n * so a drag that overshoots still highlights the part it covered.\n */\nfunction selectionWithin(container: HTMLElement): Range | null {\n  const selection = window.getSelection();\n  if (!selection || selection.rangeCount === 0 || selection.isCollapsed) return null;\n\n  const raw = selection.getRangeAt(0);\n  const startInside = container.contains(raw.startContainer);\n  const endInside = container.contains(raw.endContainer);\n  if (!startInside && !endInside) {\n    // Either the whole container sits inside the selection or none of it does.\n    const touches =\n      typeof raw.intersectsNode === 'function'\n        ? raw.intersectsNode(container)\n        : raw.commonAncestorContainer.contains(container);\n    if (!touches) return null;\n  }\n\n  const range = raw.cloneRange();\n  if (!startInside) range.setStart(container, 0);\n  if (!endInside) range.setEnd(container, container.childNodes.length);\n  if (range.collapsed) return null;\n  return range;\n}\n\nexport function captureTextSelection(container: HTMLElement): { start: number; end: number; text: string } | null {\n  const range = selectionWithin(container);\n  if (!range) return null;\n\n  const selectedText = range.toString();\n  if (!selectedText.trim()) return null;\n\n  // ── Accurate offset mapping via data-para-start annotations ──────────────\n  // HighlightedText annotates each <p> / dialogue-turn body with its raw-string\n  // start offset. Within a single annotated segment there are no block boundaries,\n  // so DOM text length == raw text length — no \\n\\n drift.\n  const segments = Array.from(container.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('[data-para-start]'));\n\n  if (segments.length > 0) {\n    const segStart = (seg: HTMLElement) => parseInt(seg.getAttribute('data-para-start') ?? '0');\n\n    // Returns the character offset from the start of `seg` to `node:nodeOffset`\n    const withinOffset = (seg: HTMLElement, node: Node, nodeOffset: number): number => {\n      const r = document.createRange();\n      r.setStart(seg, 0);\n      r.setEnd(node, nodeOffset);\n      return r.toString().length;\n    };\n\n    // Find the annotated segment that contains a given DOM node\n    const findSeg = (node: Node): HTMLElement | null => {\n      for (const seg of segments) {\n        if (seg === node || seg.contains(node)) return seg;\n      }\n      return null;\n    };\n\n    // A boundary that landed outside every segment (on the question number,\n    // in padding, at the clamped container edge) snaps to the nearest segment\n    // edge inside the selection instead of falling back to a DOM-length count\n    // that would shift the whole highlight by the stray characters.\n    const segmentRange = (seg: HTMLElement) => {\n      const r = document.createRange();\n      r.selectNodeContents(seg);\n      return r;\n    };\n    const firstSegmentAfterStart = (): HTMLElement | null =>\n      segments.find((seg) => range.compareBoundaryPoints(Range.START_TO_START, segmentRange(seg)) <= 0) ?? 0);\n    }\n\n    if (rawStart !== null && rawEnd !== null && rawEnd > rawStart) {\n      return { start: rawStart, end: rawEnd, text: selectedText };\n    }\n    if (rawStart !== null && rawEnd !== null) return null;\n  }\n\n  // ── Fallback for containers without data-para-start ──────────────────────\n  const containerRange = document.createRange();\n  containerRange.selectNodeContents(container);\n  const beforeRange = containerRange.cloneRange();\n  beforeRange.setEnd(range.startContainer, range.startOffset);\n  const start = beforeRange.toString().length;\n  const end   = start + selectedText.length;\n  return { start, end, text: selectedText };\n}\n\n/**\n * One drag can cross the stem and two answer choices. Every region it touches\n * gets its own capture, each clipped to that region's text, so the student\n * gets exactly what she swept rather than only the block the mouse came up in.\n * Whitespace-only slivers (the gap between blocks) are dropped.\n */\nexport function captureSelectionAcross(regions: HighlightRegion[]): CapturedSelection[] {\n  const captured: CapturedSelection[] = [];\n  for (const region of regions) {\n    const hit = captureTextSelection(region.container);\n    if (!hit) continue;\n    const start = Math.max(0, Math.min(hit.start, region.source.length));\n    const end = Math.max(start, Math.min(hit.end, region.source.length));\n    const text = region.source.slice(start, end);\n    if (!text.trim()) continue;\n    captured.push({ section: region.section, start, end, text });\n  }\n  return captured;\n}\n\n/**\n * Add a highlight, cutting away whatever it overlaps in the same section\n * (\"last action wins\"). a.start - b.start : a.section < b.section ? -1 : 1,\n  );\n  const merged: Highlight[] = [];\n  let current = sorted[0];\n\n  for (let i = 1; i < sorted.length; i++) {\n    const next = sorted[i];\n\n    // If adjacent and same color, merge\n    if (next.start === current.end && next.color === current.color && next.section === current.section) {\n      current = {\n        ...current,\n        end: next.end,\n        text: current.text + next.text,\n      };\n    } else {\n      merged.push(current);\n      current = next;\n    }\n  }\n  merged.push(current);\n\n  return merged;\n}\n\nexport function mergeOverlappingHighlights(highlights: Highlight[]): Highlight[] {\n  if (highlights.length <= 1) return highlights;\n\n  const sorted = [...highlights].sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);\n  const merged: Highlight[] = [];\n  let current = sorted[0];\n\n  for (let i = 1; i < sorted.length; i++) {\n    const next = sorted[i];\n\n    // If overlapping or adjacent, merge and prefer the latest color\n    if (next.start <= current.end) {\n      current = {\n        ...next, // Use next highlight's properties (newer)\n        start: Math.min(current.start, next.start),\n        end: Math.max(current.end, next.end),\n      };\n    } else {\n      merged.push(current);\n      current = next;\n    }\n  }\n  merged.push(current);\n\n  return merged;\n}\n"}

One implementation, so a highlight behaves the same in all\n * of them. Nothing here is persisted; highlights live as long as the surface.\n *\n * `applySelection` takes every region the pane offers and highlights the part\n * of the live selection that falls in each, so one drag across the stem and\n * two choices marks all three.\n */\nexport function useQuestionHighlights() {\n  const [highlights, setHighlights] = React.useState<Map<string, Highlight[]>>(new Map());\n  const [history, setHistory] = React.useState<Map<string, Highlight[]>[]>([]);\n\n  const remember = React.useCallback((snapshot: Map<string, Highlight[]>) => {\n    setHistory((prev) => [...prev, new Map(snapshot)].slice(-MAX_HISTORY));\n  }, []);\n\n  const highlightsFor = React.useCallback(\n    (qid: string | undefined): Highlight[] => (qid ? highlights.get(qid) ?? [] : []),\n    [highlights],\n  );\n\n  /**\n   * Turn the live selection into highlights (or erasures) on `qid`.\n   * Returns true when something changed, which is also when the selection has\n   * been cleared; a caller can use that to tell a marking drag from a click.\n   */\n  const applySelection = React.useCallback(\n    (qid: string | undefined, mode: HighlightMode, regions: HighlightRegion[]): boolean => {\n      if (!qid || mode === 'none') return false;\n      const captured = captureSelectionAcross(regions);\n      if (captured.length === 0) return false;\n\n      let next = highlights.get(qid) ?? [];\n      for (const hit of captured) {\n        if (mode === 'erase') {\n          next = eraseRange(next, hit.section, hit.start, hit.end);\n        } else {\n          next = replaceOverlappingHighlights(next, {\n            id: crypto.randomUUID(),\n            start: hit.start,\n            end: hit.end,\n            text: hit.text,\n            color: mode as HighlightColor,\n            section: hit.section,\n          });\n        }\n      }\n\n      remember(highlights);\n      setHighlights(new Map(highlights).set(qid, next));\n      window.getSelection()?.removeAllRanges();\n      return true;\n    },\n    [highlights, remember],\n  );\n\n  /** The eraser's click on a highlight: the whole thing goes. */\n  const removeHighlight = React.useCallback(\n    (qid: string | undefined, highlightId: string) => {\n      if (!qid) return;\n      const current = highlights.get(qid) ?? [];\n      if (!current.some((h) => h.id === highlightId)) return;\n      remember(highlights);\n      setHighlights(new Map(highlights).set(qid, current.filter((h) => h.id !== highlightId)));\n    },\n    [highlights, remember],\n  );\n\n  const undo = React.useCallback(() => {\n    setHistory((prev) => {\n      if (prev.length === 0) return prev;\n      setHighlights(new Map(prev[prev.length - 1]));\n      return prev.slice(0, -1);\n    });\n  }, []);\n\n  const clearHistory = React.useCallback(() => setHistory([]), []);\n\n  return {\n    highlights,\n    highlightsFor,\n    applySelection,\n    removeHighlight,\n    undo,\n    canUndo: history.length > 0,\n    clearHistory,\n  };\n}\n\nexport type QuestionHighlights = ReturnType<typeof useQuestionHighlights>;\n"}

export default function OfficialQuestionPane({
  question,
  displayNumber,
  selectedAnswer,
  eliminatedAnswers,
  isFlagged,
  answerRevealed = false,
  correctAnswer,
  inlineNotes,
  highlights,
  highlightMode,
  searchTerm,
  fontScale = 1,
  onAnswerSelect,
  onEliminate,
  onToggleFlag,
  onTextSelection,
  onHighlightClick,
  aboveChoices,
}: Props) {
  const stimulusHighlights = highlights.filter((h) => h.section === "stimulus");
  const stemHighlights = highlights.filter((h) => h.section === "stem");
  const stimulusText = stripDataArtifacts(question.stimulus);
  const stemText = stripDataArtifacts(question.questionStem);
  const answerEntries = Object.entries(question.answerChoices).map(([key, text]) => [
    key,
    cleanAnswerChoice(key, text),
  ] as const);

  // 7Sage sets both the stimulus and the answer choices at 19px and the
  // student reads ours next to theirs, so the two match rather than land a
  // couple of pixels apart. The stimulus carries the extra weight of the serif
  // face; stem and choices stay on the sans stack at the same size.
  const baseSize = 19 * fontScale;
  const officialContentTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    color: "#111827",
    fontFamily: '"Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif',
    fontSize: `${baseSize}px`,
    fontWeight: 400,
    lineHeight: 1.6,
  };
  const stimStyle: React.CSSProperties = {
    ...officialContentTextStyle,
    fontFamily: "Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif",
    fontSize: `${19 * fontScale}px`,
    lineHeight: 1.7,
    // Fraunces is a variable font with an optical-size axis. Left on `auto` the
    // browser picks the cut from the rendered px size, which drifts as the
    // student changes fontScale. Pin it to the text-optimised end so hairlines
    // stay the same weight at every scale.
    fontOpticalSizing: "none",
    fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
  };
  const stemStyle: React.CSSProperties = officialContentTextStyle;
  // The answer-choice text and letter badges used Tailwind's `text-sm`/`text-xs`,
  // which are hard px values and silently overrode the inherited scaled size —
  // so fontScale grew the stimulus and stem while the answers stayed at 14px.
  const choiceTextStyle: React.CSSProperties = { fontSize: `${baseSize}px` };
  const choiceBadgeStyle: React.CSSProperties = { fontSize: `${baseSize * 0.78}px` };

  return (
    <div className="relative flex flex-col flex-1 min-h-0 bg-white text-slate-950">
      <DrillSplit
        ratio="passage-weighted"
        maxWidthClassName={null}
        left={
          // Stimulus column
          <div
            className={cn(
              "min-w-0 px-6 pt-6 pb-7 sm:pl-9 sm:pr-8 lg:border-r border-slate-200 whitespace-pre-wrap overflow-y-auto",
              // Cap the reading measure. The frame is max-w-[1400px] and this column
              // takes 53fr of it, which ran ~100 characters per line — well past the
              // 45-75 readable band. `ch` is the width of "0", which is narrow in
              // Fraunces, so 52ch measures out to ~68 real characters.

/**
 * LRQuestionModal — shared LR Smart Drill–styled modal for any LSAT-format
 * multiple-choice question across the app.
 *
 * Two modes:
 *   1. Bank-backed: pass { initialQid, queue } — pulls from questionBank,
 *      logs an `attempts` row on submit (mode = `attemptMode`, default
 *      "bootcamp-pf").
 *   2. Inline: pass { inline: { ...item } } — used for bootcamp content
 *      that lives outside the LSAT U bank (e.g. Capstone, Simulator).
 *      Calls `onSubmit({ key, correct })` so the host can record progress.
 */

import * as React from "react";
import { motion, AnimatePresence } from "framer-motion";
import { X, ChevronLeft, ChevronRight, CheckCircle2, XCircle } from "lucide-react";
import { toast } from "sonner";
import OfficialQuestionPane from "@/components/drill/official/OfficialQuestionPane";
import {
  type InlineChoiceNotes,
} from "@/components/drill/official/InlineChoiceExplanation";
import { useLrExplanation } from "@/hooks/useLrExplanation";
import { questionBank, type LRQuestion } from "@/lib/questionLoader";
import { useQuestionBank } from "@/contexts/QuestionBankContext";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { useClassId } from "@/hooks/useClassId";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";

export interface InlineQuestionInput {
  /** Stable id for this item (used in header + persistence). */
  id: string;
  /** Optional display number (defaults to 1). */
  number?: number | string;
  stimulus: string;
  questionStem: string;
  /** Map of letter → choice text. Any A..E subset works. */
  answerChoices: Record<string, string>;
  correctAnswer: string;
  /** Optional per-choice explanation for the post-submit panel. */
  explanations?: Partial<

  // Bank-backed nav state
  const queue = props.queue ?? [];
  const initialQid = props.initialQid ?? "";
  const startIdx = Math.max(0, queue.indexOf(initialQid));
  const [idx, setIdx] = React.useState(startIdx);
  const qid = isInline ? props.inline!.id : queue[idx] ?? initialQid;

  const [bankQuestion, setBankQuestion] = React.useState<LRQuestion | undefined>(undefined);
  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!isInline) setBankQuestion(questionBank.getQuestion(qid));
  }, [qid, qbLoading, isInline]);

  const inline = isInline ? props.inline! : null;

  // Normalized question for OfficialQuestionPane
  const renderQuestion = React.useMemo(() => {
    if (inline) {
      return {
        qid: inline.id,
        qnum: inline.number,
        stimulus: inline.stimulus || "",
        questionStem: inline.questionStem || "",
        answerChoices: inline.answerChoices,
      };
    }
    if (!bankQuestion) return null;
    return {
      qid: bankQuestion.qid,
      qnum: bankQuestion.qnum,
      stimulus: bankQuestion.stimulus || "",
      questionStem: bankQuestion.questionStem,
      answerChoices: bankQuestion.answerChoices,
    };
  }, [inline, bankQuestion]);

  const correctAnswer = inline?.correctAnswer ?? bankQuestion?.correctAnswer ?? "";
  const explanations = inline?.explanations ?? bankQuestion?.answerChoiceExplanations;

  const [selected, setSelected] = React.useState("");
  const [eliminated, setEliminated] = React.useState<Set<string>>(new Set());
  const [submitted, setSubmitted] = React.useState(false);
  const startTsRef = React.useRef<number>(Date.now());

  React.useEffect(() => {
    setSelected("");
    setEliminated(new Set());
    setSubmitted(false);
    startTsRef.current = Date.now();
  }, [qid]);

  React.useEffect(() => {
    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => { if (e.key === "Escape") props.onClose(); };
    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
  }, [props.onClose]);
                  >
                    <ChevronRight className="w-5 h-5" />
                  </button>
                </>
              )}
              <button
                onClick={props.onClose}
                className="ml-1 p-1.5 rounded hover:bg-slate-200 active:scale-[0.92] transition-transform duration-100"
                aria-label="Close"
              >
                <X className="w-5 h-5" />
              </button>
            </div>
          </div>

          {/* Body */}
          <div className="flex-1 overflow-y-auto">
            {loading || !renderQuestion ? (
              <div className="p-12 text-center text-slate-500">
                {qbLoading ? "Loading question bank…" : "This question is not in the LSAT U bank yet."}
              </div>
            ) : (
              <>
                <OfficialQuestionPane
                  question={renderQuestion}
                  displayNumber={Number(renderQuestion.qnum) || idx + 1}
                  selectedAnswer={selected}
                  eliminatedAnswers={eliminated}
                  isFlagged={false}
                  answerRevealed={submitted}
                  correctAnswer={correctAnswer}
                  inlineNotes={inlineNotes}
                  highlights={[]}
                  highlightMode="none"
                  onAnswerSelect={(k) => !submitted && setSelected(k)}
                  onEliminate={handleEliminate}
                  onToggleFlag={() => {}}
                  onTextSelection={() => {}}
                  onHighlightClick={() => {}}
                />

                {submitted && (
                  <div className="px-6 sm:px-8 pb-8">
                    <div
                      className={`flex items-center gap-2 p-3 rounded-lg border ${
                        correct
                          ? "bg-emerald-50 border-emerald-200 text-emerald-800"
                          : "bg-rose-50 border-rose-200 text-rose-800"
                      }`}
                    >
                      {correct ? <CheckCircle2 className="w-5 h-5" /> : <XCircle className="w-5 h-5" />}
                      <span className="font-semibold">
                        {correct ? "Correct" : `Incorrect — answer is ${correctAnswer}`}
                      </span>
                    </div>
                    {/* Per-choice explanations render inline under each choice
                        above, not as a separate block down here. */}
                  </div>
                )}
              </>
            )}

        highlights={[]}

        {...props}
      />,
    );
  }

  it("marks the choice they actually took, not only the one that was right", () => {
    renderRevealed();

    // B was chosen and is wrong; saying "A is correct" without saying "and you
    // took B" is only half of what a reveal is for.
    expect(
      screen.getByRole("button", {
        name: /There has been an increase in ratings/i,
      }),
    ).toHaveClass("text-red-900");
  });

  it("does not leave the verdict to colour alone", () => {
    // ADR 0001: feedback must always carry an icon and a label, so a student who
    // cannot separate the green from the red still gets the answer.
    renderRevealed();

    expect(screen.getByLabelText("Correct answer")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByLabelText("Your answer, incorrect")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("shows the answer without unfolding every explanation", () => {
    // Answer and Explanation are separate asks. Revealing with no inline notes
    // passed must colour the choices and add nothing else.
    renderRevealed({ inlineNotes: null });

    expect(screen.getByLabelText("Correct answer")).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText(/why this is/i)).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("unfolds them when the notes are handed over, which is Explanation's job", () => {
    // The other half of the pair: without this, the assertion above would pass
    // just as happily if the inline layer had been deleted outright.
    renderRevealed({
      inlineNotes: {
        A: { tag: null, studentPercent: null, note: "A is the only choice that explains it." },
        B: { tag: null, studentPercent: null, note: "B changes the subject." },
      },
    });

    expect(screen.getByText(/why this is correct/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText(/why this is wrong/i)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("leaves an untaken wrong choice alone", () => {
    // Only the choice they committed to turns red. The other wrong answers are
    // not accusations.
    renderRevealed({ selectedAnswer: "A" });

    expect(
      screen.getByRole("button", {
        name: /There has been an increase in ratings/i,
      }),
    ).not.toHaveClass("text-red-900");
    expect(screen.queryByLabelText("Your answer, incorrect")).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });
});

start: number,\n  end: number,\n  section: Highlight['section'],\n  color: Highlight['color'] = 'yellow',\n  text = 'x'.repeat(end - start),\n): Highlight => ({ id, start, end, text, color, section document.body.innerHTML = '';\n  });\n\n  function mount(html: string): HTMLElement {\n    document.body.innerHTML = html;\n    return document.body.firstElementChild as HTMLElement;\n  }\n\n  function select(startNode: Node, startOffset: number, endNode: Node, endOffset: number) {\n    const range = document.createRange();\n    range.setStart(startNode, startOffset);\n    range.setEnd(endNode, endOffset);\n    const selection = window.getSelection()!;\n    selection.removeAllRanges();\n    selection.addRange(range);\n  }\n\n  it('maps a selection inside an annotated 6, text, 9);\n    expect(captureTextSelection(el)).toEqual({ start: 6, end: 9, text: 'one' });\n  });\n\n  it('snaps a start on the question number to the start of the stem', () => {\n    // The \"26.\" lives beside the stem, outside the container. delta</p></div></div>';\n    const a = document.querySelector('.a') as HTMLElement;\n    const b = document.querySelector('.b') as HTMLElement;\n    const aText = a.querySelector('p')!.firstChild!;\n expect(captureTextSelection(b)).toMatchObject({ start: 0, end: 5 });\n  });\n\n  it('returns null for a container the selection never touched', () => {\n    document.body.innerHTML =\n      '<div><div class=\"a\"><p data-para-start=\"0\">alpha</p></div><div class=\"b\"><p data-para-start=\"0\">beta</p></div></div>';\n    const a = document.querySelector('.a') as HTMLElement;\n    const b = });\n});\n\ndescribe('captureSelectionAcross', () => {\n  afterEach(() => {\n    window.getSelection()?.removeAllRanges();\n    document.body.innerHTML = '';\n  });\n\n  it('gives every touched region all</p></div></div>';\n    const stem = document.querySelector('.stem') as HTMLElement;\n    const a = document.querySelector('.a') as HTMLElement;\n    const range = document.createRange();\n {"command": "cd \"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch\" && git status --short && git add src/lib/highlightUtils.ts src/lib/highlightUtils.test.ts src/hooks/useQuestionHighlights.ts src/hooks/useQuestionHighlights.test.ts src/components/drill/HighlightedText.tsx src/components/drill/official/OfficialQuestionPane.tsx src/components/drill/official/OfficialQuestionPane.test.tsx src/components/drill/EnhancedBlindReview.tsx src/components/drill/SectionReviewWalk.tsx src/components/drill/shared/LRQuestionModal.tsx src/pages/Drill.tsx && git commit -q -m \"Highlight and underline the stem and every answer choice, on every LR surface\n\nAnswer choices were rendered as buttons, and a browser will not start a\ndrag-selection inside a button, so the marker tools only ever reached the\nstimulus. The choice text is now a plain block that still picks the\nchoice on a click and never on a drag; the letter badge stays the\nkeyboard control.\n\nA stem highlight also used to vanish the moment the stimulus was\nhighlighted: overlap was tested by offset alone, with no section check,\nso offset 0 of the stem and offset 0 of the stimulus fought each other.\nEvery section, each choice included, now has its own lane.\n\nOne drag can cross the stem and two choices; each block gets its own\nmark. A selection that starts on the question number snaps to the start\nof the stem instead of shifting the mark by four characters.\n\nHighlight state moves into useQuestionHighlights so the drill, blind\nreview, the section walk and the bootcamp capstone modal behave the same.\nBlind review and the capstone modal had the tools but no handler.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\" && git log --oneline -1", "description": "Commit the LR highlight work"}

## user

Tool result:
"risks": "1) Latent, pre-existing, and left alone per scope: painting splits the text nodes React rendered; when React later patches that text in place (same paragraph, different string) it updates only the first fragment and leaves the mark and tail behind, and clearMarks' normalize() then merges the garbage. I keyed the stem and choice wrappers on questionId so the question pane never takes that path, but RcPassagePane still can when a multi-passage session switches passages and both have marks in the same paragraph position. A key on the passage text div would close it; not done because it is outside the brief. 2) Click on an existing mark in a choice with no tool active both opens the Remove popover and answers with that choice (the click is collapsed and the mode is none, exactly the rule the design asked for). If that double effect is unwanted, the fix is a one-line ref set in the hook's remove rung and read in clickText. 3) React 18 event ordering: my analysis that the Remove button used to die before its click is reasoned, not observed; verify step 9 above covers it. 4) The question pane's popover dismiss runs on any mouse-up in the pane outside a surface or the popover; the passage pane's popover is separate state, so a selection in one pane leaves the other pane's popover open until its own dismiss. 5) rc_annotations rows for a passage now grow with every question's marks; there is no cap, same as before."

{"command": "cd \"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch\" && sed -n 1,30p src/features/rc/components/RcChoiceRow.tsx | grep -n \"import\" && python - <<'PYEOF'\np='src/features/rc/components/RcChoiceRow.tsx'\ns=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()\nold=\"\"\"  const clickText = () => {\n    if (highlightMode !== 'none') return;\n    const selection = window.getSelection();\n    if (selection && !selection.isCollapsed) return;\n    pick();\n  };\n\"\"\"\nnew=\"\"\"  const clickText = (event: React.MouseEvent<HTMLElement>) => {\n    if (highlightMode !== 'none') return;\n    const selection = window.getSelection();\n    if (selection && !selection.isCollapsed) return;\n    // A click on an existing mark is aimed at the mark (it opens the Remove\n    // popover), not at the answer.\n    if ((event.target as HTMLElement).closest?.(`[${RC_HIGHLIGHT_ATTR}]`)) return;\n    pick();\n  };\n\"\"\"\nassert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new,1)\nif 'RC_HIGHLIGHT_ATTR' not in s.split('const clickText')[0]:\n    # add import\n    import re\n    m=re.search(r\"import \\{([^}]*)\\} from '../lib/rcHighlightPaint';\", s)\n    if m:\n        s=s.replace(m.group(0), \"import {\"+m.group(1).rstrip()+\", RC_HIGHLIGHT_ATTR } from '../lib/rcHighlightPaint';\",1)\n    else:\n        # insert after first import line\n        idx=s.index('\\n', s.index('import '))+1\n        s=s[:idx]+\"import { RC_HIGHLIGHT_ATTR } from '../lib/rcHighlightPaint';\\n\"+s[idx:]\nopen(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='\\n').write(s)\nprint('ok')\nPYEOF\ngrep -n \"rcHighlightPaint\" src/features/rc/components/RcChoiceRow.tsx; npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json 2>&1 | grep -v \"supabase/functions\" | head", "description": "Guard clicks on marks in RC choices; typecheck"}

          <span className="min-w-[20px] shrink-0 font-bold tabular-nums">{item.number}.</span>
          {/* Unexplained questions carry no authored prompt. The number stands

          <div
            key={question.questionId}

Review the commits in range ${RANGE} (git show / git diff ${RANGE}). Context: LSAT U (React 18 + Vite + TS, strict off). The change lets a student highlight/underline the question stem and each answer choice, not only the stimulus, in the LR drill (OfficialQuestionPane + useQuestionHighlights + highlightUtils) and in the RC drill (useRcMarkSurface, paintRcAnnotations, RcQuestionPane, RcChoiceRow, question-scoped targets q<id>:stem / q<id>:<letter> persisted in rc_annotations jsonb). Already verified live in a browser: LR stimulus/stem/choice marks coexist, plain click picks a choice, a drag never does, eraser click and drag work, a drag spanning stem into a choice marks both; RC stem and choice marks apply, plain click answers, marks survive reload. Do not re-report those as unverified. You may run vitest (npx vitest run <path>) and tsc (npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json, ignoring supabase/functions) to test hypotheses. Report only defects you can point to in the code with a concrete failing scenario; no style commentary. Return the structured findings.`\n\nconst DIMENSIONS = [\n  { key: 'lr-correctness', prompt: `${common}\n\nLens: LR correctness. Read src/lib/highlightUtils.ts, src/hooks/useQuestionHighlights.ts, src/components/drill/official/OfficialQuestionPane.tsx, src/components/drill/HighlightedText.tsx and the Drill.tsx hunks. Hunt for: offset bugs in captureTextSelection (clamping, snapping to segments, the fallback path, dialogue stimuli with data-para-start on spans, multi-paragraph stimuli where source has \\\\n\\\\n but DOM does not), a selection that starts in the stimulus column and ends in the question column, highlights whose text no longer equals source.slice(start,end) after split/merge, eraseRange edge cases (zero-width, exact-boundary), undo semantics after the hook refactor (history cleared on question change in Drill.tsx L~1309, is that still right for the other surfaces), Strict Mode double-invocation hazards, and whether crypto.randomUUID is available in every target browser the app supports.` },\n  { key: 'lr-interaction', prompt: `${common}\n\nLens: LR interaction and accessibility. Focus on OfficialQuestionPane.tsx's choice block (the text left its <button>). Hunt for: any path where a plain click no longer picks the choice (revealed, eliminated, inline explanation open, aboveChoices tabs in blind review, tutor overlay), any path where a drag still picks it (markedRef timing across mouseup/click when the mouse-up lands on a different element than the click, e.g. a drag that ends over the eye button or the badge, a drag that ends outside the pane entirely so the root onMouseUp never fires and markedRef stays stale for the NEXT click), touch devices (no drag-select but taps), keyboard access now that the text is not focusable, the aria-describedby id colliding when two panes render the same qid (blind review Actual/BR, or the modal), the eraser-mode click guard swallowing answer clicks after the student forgets the eraser is on, and cursor/hover affordance regressions. Also check the other three callers compile AND behave: EnhancedBlindReview (readOnly Actual tab: should marks still be addable?), SectionReviewWalk, LRQuestionModal (toolbar inside a modal with its own scroll: layout, Escape key, Aa popover z-index).` },\n  { key: 'rc-correctness', prompt: `${common}\n\nLens: RC correctness and persistence. Read src/features/rc/lib/rcTypes.ts, hooks/useRcHighlights.ts, hooks/useRcMarkSurface.ts, lib/rcHighlightPaint.ts, components/RcPassagePane.tsx, RcQuestionPane.tsx, RcChoiceRow.tsx, pages/RcPlayer.tsx and api/rcProgress.ts. Hunt for: the passage pane no longer behaving byte-identically (the implementer admitted three deviations: an early return when mouse-up target is inside .rc-selection-popover, popover anchor now adds scrollTop/scrollLeft, keyed wrappers), old rc_annotations rows with any shape that now fail a type or filter, sameLayer/merge/trim logic mixing targets, the 1s debounced save dropping a mark when navigating between questions of the same passage, paint effects that miss a re-render (reveal layer mount, Passage Only toggle, question change within a passage, text scale change re-rendering RcMarkdown), marks applied to a stale questionId after fast navigation, the keyed wrapper unmounting/remounting on every render if the key is unstable, and the review-only / re-attempt modes.` },\n  { key: 'rc-interaction', prompt: `${common}\n\nLens: RC interaction. Focus on RcChoiceRow.tsx and RcQuestionPane.tsx. Hunt for: any path where a plain click on choice text no longer answers, any path where a drag still answers, the click-on-mark guard (closest [data-rc-highlight]) failing because marks are <mark> elements painted after React render and may be replaced, the popover: two panes each with their own anchor state (can two popovers be open at once? does a click in the passage pane dismiss the question pane's popover? does the popover render inside the scrolled column and clip?), eliminated/revealed rows (disabled styling vs still markable), keyboard access (badge button), the eye button's stopPropagation, the five accessibility colour schemes in player.css/rc.css against mixBlendMode multiply marks on bg-slate-100 / rc-choice-correct backgrounds, and RcChoiceDisclosure children mounting under the text after reveal.` },\n  { key: 'tests-and-types', prompt: `${common}\n\nLens: tests and type safety. Run npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json (ignore supabase/functions) and npx vitest run src/lib/highlightUtils src/hooks/useQuestionHighlights src/components/drill src/features/rc src/pages. Then read the new tests (src/lib/highlightUtils.test.ts, src/hooks/useQuestionHighlights.test.ts, OfficialQuestionPane.test.tsx additions, src/features/rc/**/*.test.ts*) and identify assertions that would pass even if the feature were broken, behaviours that the browser verification covered but no test does (and would regress silently), and any test that depends on jsdom quirks (Range.intersectsNode, compareBoundaryPoints, getBoundingClientRect stubs) in a way that hides a real-browser difference. Also grep for any remaining consumer of the old signatures: onTextSelection(e, section), Highlight.section typed as 'stimulus' | 'stem', RcHighlight.target typed as 'passage' | RcLetter, including src/references/ and src/bootcamps/ (report but mark as out of scope if under src/references).` },\n]\n\nphase('Review')\nconst results = await pipeline(\n  DIMENSIONS,\n  d => agent(d.prompt, { label: `review:${d.key}`, phase: 'Review', schema: FINDINGS }),\n  (review, d) => {\n    if (!review) return []\n    const worth = review.findings.filter(f => f.severity !== 'nit')\n    log(`${d.key}: ${review.findings.length} findings, ${worth.length} worth verifying`)\n    return parallel(worth.map(f => () =>\n      agent(`${common}\n\nYou are the skeptic. A reviewer claims this defect in the range above:\nTITLE: ${f.title}\nFILE: ${f.file}:${f.line}\nSEVERITY CLAIMED: ${f.severity}\nSCENARIO: ${f.scenario}\nEVIDENCE: ${f.evidence}\nPROPOSED FIX: ${f.fix}\n\nTry to REFUTE it. Read the actual code at that location and everything it depends on. If the scenario cannot happen, or is pre-existing behaviour unchanged by this range, or is already handled elsewhere, say real=false and explain precisely. If it is real, confirm with the exact code path and rate its true severity. Default to real=false when uncertain. Return the verdict.`, { label: `verify:${d.key}:${f.title.slice(0, 40)}`, phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT })\n        .then(v => ({ ...f, dimension: d.key, verdict: v }))))\n  },\n)\n\nconst all = results.flat().filter(Boolean)\nconst confirmed = all.filter(f => f.verdict?.real)\nconst refuted = all.filter(f => f.verdict && !f.verdict.real)\nlog(`${confirmed.length} confirmed, ${refuted.length} refuted`)\nreturn { confirmed, refuted: refuted.map(f => ({ title: f.title, file: f.file, reason: f.verdict.reason })) }"}

/**
 * The current selection expressed as offsets into `container`'s rendered text.
 * Returns null when there is no selection, it is collapsed, or it falls outside.
 */
export function captureRenderedSelection(
  container: HTMLElement,
): { start: number; end: number; text: string } | null {
  const selection = window.getSelection();
  if (!selection || selection.rangeCount === 0 || selection.isCollapsed) return null;

  const range = selection.getRangeAt(0);
  if (!container.contains(range.commonAncestorContainer)) return null;

  const text = selection.toString();
  if (!text.trim()) return null;

  const before = document.createRange();
  before.selectNodeContents(container);
  before.setEnd(range.startContainer, range.startOffset);
  const start = before.toString().length;

  return { start, end: start + text.length, text };
}

/** Removes marks previously painted with the given attribute, restoring the text. */
export function clearMarks(container: HTMLElement, attr: string): void {
  const marks = Array.from(container.querySelectorAll(`mark[${attr}]`));
  for (const mark of marks) {
    const parent = mark.parentNode;
    if (!parent) continue;
    while (mark.firstChild) parent.insertBefore(mark.firstChild, mark);
    parent.removeChild(mark);
  }
  container.normalize();
}

/**
 * Wraps each range in a <mark>. Ranges are in rendered-text coordinates.
 * Existing marks with the same attribute are cleared first, so this is
====
import * as React from 'react';
import type { RcSelectionAnchor } from '../components/RcSelectionPopover';
import { RC_HIGHLIGHT_ATTR, captureRenderedSelection } from '../lib/rcHighlightPaint';
import type { RcAnnotationKind, RcHighlightColor, RcHighlightMode } from '../lib/rcTypes';

/**
 * The mouse-up ladder for one markable surface: the passage, a question stem,
 * or an answer choice. Every surface runs the same ladder so the toolbar tools,
 * the eraser and the selection popover behave identically wherever the student
 * releases the mouse.
 *
 *   erase          -> drag clears the sweep, click clears the mark under it
 *   tool active    -> a selection is applied on release, no popover
 *   click on mark  -> "Remove highlight" popover
 *   selection      -> "Highlight" popover
 *
 * The popover itself belongs to the pane, since a pane with several surfaces
 * (a stem and five choices) shows one popover. `onAnchor` hands the pane the
 * position together with a handle back to the surface that asked, so the
 * popover's buttons act on the right text.
 */

export interface RcMarkSurfaceHandle {
  /** Popover "Highlight": paint the selection the last mouse-up captured. */
  applyPending: (chosen: RcHighlightColor) => void;
  /** Popover "Remove highlight": drop the mark the last mouse-up landed on. */
  removePending: () => void;
}

export interface RcMarkSurfaceOptions {
  /** The element whose rendered text the offsets are measured against. */
  textRef: React.RefObject<HTMLElement | null>;
  /** The positioned, scrolling ancestor the popover is anchored inside. */
  paneRef: React.RefObject<HTMLElement | null>;
  highlightMode: RcHighlightMode;
  color: RcHighlightColor;
  onAdd: (start: number, end: number, color: RcHighlightColor, kind: RcAnnotationKind) => void;
  onRemove: (offset: number) => void;
  onRemoveRange: (start: number, end: number) => void;
  onAnchor: (anchor: RcSelectionAnchor | null, surface: RcMarkSurfaceHandle) => void;
}

/** Rendered-text offset of the first character inside a painted mark. */
function offsetOfMark(el: HTMLElement, mark: Element): number {
  const before = document.createRange();
  before.selectNodeContents(el);
  before.setEnd(mark, 0);
  return before.toString().length;
}

export function useRcMarkSurface(options: RcMarkSurfaceOptions) {
  // The handle is created once and read by the pane's popover later, so it
  // reads the options through a ref rather than closing over one render's.
  const latest = React.useRef(options);
  latest.current = options;

  const pending = React.useRef<{ start: number; end: number } | null>(null);
  const removeOffset = React.useRef<number | null>(null);

  const handle = React.useMemo<RcMarkSurfaceHandle>(() => {
    const self: RcMarkSurfaceHandle = {
      applyPending: (chosen) => {
        if (pending.current) {
          latest.current.onAdd(pending.current.start, pending.current.end, chosen, 'highlight');
        }
        window.getSelection()?.removeAllRanges();
        latest.current.onAnchor(null, self);
      },
      removePending: () => {
        if (removeOffset.current != null) latest.current.onRemove(removeOffset.current);
        latest.current.onAnchor(null, self);
      },
    };
    return self;
  }, []);

  /**
   * Popover position, in the pane's content coordinates. The pane scrolls and
   * the popover is positioned inside it, so the scroll offset is added back:
   * client rects are measured against the viewport, not the scrolled content.
   */
  const anchorFor = (rect: DOMRect, mode: RcSelectionAnchor['mode']): RcSelectionAnchor => {
    const pane = latest.current.paneRef.current!;
    const paneRect = pane.getBoundingClientRect();
    return {
      x: rect.left - paneRect.left + pane.scrollLeft,
      y: rect.bottom - paneRect.top + 6 + pane.scrollTop,
      mode,
    };
  };

  const onMouseUp = (event: React.MouseEvent) => {
    const { textRef, highlightMode, color, onAdd, onRemove, onRemoveRange, onAnchor } =
      latest.current;
    const el = textRef.current;
    if (!el) return;

    const target = event.target as HTMLElement;
    // A release on the popover's own buttons is the click that is about to
    // fire. Treating it as a release on the text would dismiss the popover
    // first, and the button would be gone before its click arrived.
    if (target.closest('.rc-selection-popover')) return;

    const mark = target.closest(`mark[${RC_HIGHLIGHT_ATTR}]`);
    const selection = captureRenderedSelection(el);

    // With the eraser active, a click clears the mark under it and a drag
    // clears everything the sweep touches. No popover either way.
    if (highlightMode === 'erase') {
      if (selection) {
        onRemoveRange(selection.start, selection.end);
        window.getSelection()?.removeAllRanges();
      } else if (mark) {
        onRemove(offsetOfMark(el, mark));
      }
      onAnchor(null, handle);
      return;
    }

    // A marker or the underline is active: apply on release, like the official
    // interface, rather than asking again through the popover.
    if (highlightMode !== 'none' && selection) {
      const kind: RcAnnotationKind = highlightMode === 'underline' ? 'underline' : 'highlight';
      onAdd(
        selection.start,
        selection.end,
        kind === 'underline' ? color : (highlightMode as RcHighlightColor),
        kind,
      );
      window.getSelection()?.removeAllRanges();
      onAnchor(null, handle);
      return;
    }

    if (!selection && mark) {
      // Clicking an existing highlight offers to remove it.
      removeOffset.current = offsetOfMark(el, mark);
      onAnchor(anchorFor(mark.getBoundingClientRect(), 'remove'), handle);
      return;
    }

    if (!selection) {
      onAnchor(null, handle);
      return;
    }

    pending.current = { start: selection.start, end: selection.end };
    const rect = window.getSelection()?.getRangeAt(0)?.getBoundingClientRect();
    if (rect) onAnchor(anchorFor(rect, 'apply'), handle);
  };

  return { onMouseUp, ...handle };
}

export type RcMarkSurface = ReturnType<typeof useRcMarkSurface>;

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch\\src\\hooks\\useDeferredPick.ts", "content": "import * as React from 'react';\n\n/**\n * How long to wait before treating a click on answer text as a pick while a\n * marker tool is active. A double-click to mark one word arrives as two full\n * click cycles, and the word is only selected on the second press, so the\n * first click cannot tell itself apart from a real pick. Waiting this long\n * lets the second press cancel it. Only marking mode pays the wait.\n */\nexport const DEFERRED_PICK_MS = 300;\n\n/**\n * A pick that can still be called off. `schedule` replaces any pending pick;\n * `cancel` drops it; unmount drops it too, so a pick never lands on a row\n * that is gone.\n */\nexport function useDeferredPick() {\n  const timer = React.useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);\n\n  const cancel = React.useCallback(() => {\n    if (timer.current !== null) {\n      clearTimeout(timer.current);\n      timer.current = null;\n    }\n  }, []);\n\n  const schedule = React.useCallback(\n    (pick: () => void, delayMs: number = DEFERRED_PICK_MS) => {\n      cancel();\n      timer.current = setTimeout(() => {\n        timer.current = null;\n        pick();\n      }, delayMs);\n    },\n    [cancel],\n  );\n\n  React.useEffect(() => cancel, [cancel]);\n\n  return { schedule, cancel };\n}\n"}

/**
 * Painting highlights and find-text matches onto already-rendered markdown.
 *
 * Highlights are stored as character offsets, never as DOM nodes, so they
 * survive a reload and can be restored on any future encounter with a passage.
 * The offsets are measured against the *rendered* text of the container rather
 * than the raw markdown source: markdown syntax (the asterisks around
 * **Passage A**) never appears on screen, so an offset into the raw string does
 * not line up with anything the student can select. Rendering is deterministic
 * from the same source, so rendered offsets round-trip reliably.
 *
 * Painting walks text nodes and wraps the overlapping slice of each one, which
 * lets a highlight span element boundaries (a range crossing a bold run, say)
 * without needing the range to be well-formed for surroundContents.
 */

import { rcHighlightColor, type RcHighlight } from './rcTypes';

export const RC_HIGHLIGHT_ATTR = 'data-rc-highlight';
export const RC_FIND_ATTR = 'data-rc-find';

export interface RcTextRange {
  start: number;
  end: number;
  color?: string;
  /** 'highlight' or 'underline'; read by the caller's styleFor. */
  kind?: string;
}

/** Every text node under `root`, in document order. */
function textNodes(root: HTMLElement): Text[] {
  const walker = document.createTreeWalker(root, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);
  const nodes: Text[] = [];
  let node = walker.nextNode();
  while (node) {
    nodes.push(node as Text);
    node = walker.nextNode();
  }
  return nodes;
}
 * Existing marks with the same attribute are cleared first, so this is
 * idempotent and safe to call on every render.
 */
export function paintRanges(
  container: HTMLElement,
  ranges: RcTextRange[],
  attr: string,
  styleFor?: (range: RcTextRange) => Partial<CSSStyleDeclaration>,
): void {
  clearMarks(container, attr);
  if (ranges.length === 0) return;

  for (const range of ranges) {
    if (range.end <= range.start) continue;
    // Re-walk per range: painting mutates the tree, so offsets must be
    // recomputed against the current DOM each time.
    let offset = 0;
    for (const node of textNodes(container)) {
      const length = node.data.length;
      const nodeStart = offset;
      const nodeEnd = offset + length;
      offset = nodeEnd;

      if (nodeEnd <= range.start || nodeStart >= range.end) continue;

      const from = Math.max(0, range.start - nodeStart);
      const to = Math.min(length, range.end - nodeStart);
      if (to <= from) continue;

      // Split the node so the middle piece is exactly the overlap.
      const middle = from > 0 ? node.splitText(from) : node;
      if (to - from < middle.data.length) middle.splitText(to - from);

      const mark = document.createElement('mark');
      mark.setAttribute(attr, range.color ?? 'true');
      const style = styleFor?.(range);
      if (style) Object.assign(mark.style, style);
      middle.parentNode?.insertBefore(mark, middle);
      mark.appendChild(middle);
    }
  }
}

/**
 * Paints a student's stored marks onto one surface: the passage, a stem, or a
 * choice. One styling for all of them, so a mark looks the same wherever it
 * sits. Callers pass only the marks that belong to this surface.
 */
export function paintRcAnnotations(container: HTMLElement, highlights: RcHighlight[]): void {
  paintRanges(
    container,

{"command": "cd \"C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu-fix-homework-timed-launch\" && python - <<'PYEOF'\np='src/features/rc/lib/rcHighlightPaint.ts'\ns=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()\nold='''/**\n * The current selection expressed as offsets into `container`'s rendered text.\n * Returns null when there is no selection, it is collapsed, or it falls outside.\n */\nexport function captureRenderedSelection(\n  container: HTMLElement,\n): { start: number; end: number; text: string } | null {\n  const selection = window.getSelection();\n  if (!selection || selection.rangeCount === 0 || selection.isCollapsed) return null;\n\n  const range = selection.getRangeAt(0);\n  if (!container.contains(range.commonAncestorContainer)) return null;\n\n  const text = selection.toString();\n  if (!text.trim()) return null;\n\n  const before = document.createRange();\n  before.selectNodeContents(container);\n  before.setEnd(range.startContainer, range.startOffset);\n  const start = before.toString().length;\n\n  return { start, end: start + text.length, text };\n}'''\nnew='''/**\n * The current selection expressed as offsets into `container`'s rendered text.\n * Returns null when there is no selection, it is collapsed, or it never\n * touches the container.\n *\n * A selection that starts before the container or runs past it is cut at the\n * container's edges rather than rejected: a drag that overshoots the end of a\n * choice into the row's padding, or sweeps from the stem into a choice, still\n * marks the part of this surface it covered. Lengths are measured with\n * Range.toString, which concatenates text nodes exactly as painting walks\n * them; Selection.toString adds a newline at every block edge and would push\n * a multi-paragraph mark past where the drag ended.\n */\nexport function captureRenderedSelection(\n  container: HTMLElement,\n): { start: number; end: number; text: string } | null {\n  const selection = window.getSelection();\n  if (!selection || selection.rangeCount === 0 || selection.isCollapsed) return null;\n\n  const raw = selection.getRangeAt(0);\n  const startInside = container.contains(raw.startContainer);\n  const endInside = container.contains(raw.endContainer);\n  if (!startInside && !endInside) {\n    const touches =\n      typeof raw.intersectsNode === 'function'\n        ? raw.intersectsNode(container)\n        : raw.commonAncestorContainer.contains(container);\n    if (!touches) return null;\n  }\n\n  const range = raw.cloneRange();\n  if (!startInside) range.setStart(container, 0);\n  if (!endInside) range.setEnd(container, container.childNodes.length);\n  if (range.collapsed) return null;\n\n  const text = range.toString();\n  if (!text.trim()) return null;\n\n  const before = document.createRange();\n  before.selectNodeContents(container);\n  before.setEnd(range.startContainer, range.startOffset);\n  const start = before.toString().length;\n\n  return { start, end: start + text.length, text };\n}'''\nassert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new,1)\nopen(p,'w',encoding='utf-8',newline='\\n').write(s)\nprint('ok')\nPYEOF", "description": "Clamp RC selection capture to the surface"}

Every surface runs the same ladder so the toolbar tools,\n * the eraser and the selection popover behave identically wherever the student\n * releases the mouse.\n *\n *   erase          -> drag clears the sweep, click clears the mark under it\n *   tool active    -> a selection is applied on release, no popover\n *   click on mark  -> \"Remove highlight\" popover\n *   selection      -> \"Highlight\" popover\n *\n * The surface listens on the document, not on its own element. A drag that\n * runs past the end of a choice is released on the row's padding, and a drag\n * from the stem into a choice is released on the choice; either way the\n * surface still owns the part of the selection that fell inside it, and it\n * takes exactly that part (captureRenderedSelection clips to the surface).\n * A release with no selection anywhere puts every popover away, so two panes\n * never show one each.\n *\n * The popover itself belongs to the pane, since a pane with several surfaces\n * (a stem and five choices) shows one popover. `onAnchor` hands the pane the\n * position together with a handle back to the surface that asked, so the\n * popover's buttons act on the right text.\n */\n\nexport interface RcMarkSurfaceHandle {\n  /** Popover \"Highlight\": paint the selection the last mouse-up captured. */\n  applyPending: (chosen: RcHighlightColor) => void;\n  /** Popover \"Remove highlight\": drop the mark the last mouse-up landed on. */\n  removePending: () => void;\n  /**\n   * Whether the most recent mouse-up on the document was a marking gesture\n   * on this surface (a mark applied, erased, or offered for removal). The\n   * click that follows such a mouse-up is not a pick.\n   */\n  consumedLastMouseUp: () => boolean;\n}\n\nexport interface RcMarkSurfaceOptions {\n  /** The element whose rendered text the offsets are measured against. */\n  textRef: React.RefObject<HTMLElement | null>;\n  /** The positioned, scrolling ancestor the popover is anchored inside. */\n  paneRef: React.RefObject<HTMLElement | null>;\n  highlightMode: RcHighlightMode;\n  color: RcHighlightColor;\n  onAdd: (start: number, end: number, color: RcHighlightColor, kind: RcAnnotationKind) => void;\n  onRemove: (offset: number) => void;\n  onRemoveRange: (start: number, end: number) => void;\n  onAnchor: (anchor: RcSelectionAnchor | null, surface: RcMarkSurfaceHandle) => void;\n}\n\n/** Rendered-text offset of the first character inside a painted mark. */\nfunction offsetOfMark(el: HTMLElement, mark: Element): number {\n  const before = document.createRange();\n  before.selectNodeContents(el);\n  before.setEnd(mark, 0);\n  return before.toString().length;\n}\n\nexport function useRcMarkSurface(options: RcMarkSurfaceOptions): RcMarkSurfaceHandle {\n  // The handle is created once and read by the pane's popover later, so it\n  // reads the options through a ref rather than closing over one render's.\n  const latest = React.useRef(options);\n  latest.current = options;\n\n  const pending = React.useRef<{ start: number; end: number } | null>(null);\n  const removeOffset = React.useRef<number | null>(null);\n  const consumed = React.useRef(false);\n\n  const handle = React.useMemo<RcMarkSurfaceHandle>(() => {\n    const self: RcMarkSurfaceHandle = {\n      applyPending: (chosen) => {\n        if (pending.current) {\n          latest.current.onAdd(pending.current.start, pending.current.end, chosen, 'highlight');\n          pending.current = null;\n        }\n        window.getSelection()?.removeAllRanges();\n        latest.current.onAnchor(null, self);\n      },\n      removePending: () => {\n        if (removeOffset.current != null) latest.current.onRemove(removeOffset.current);\n        removeOffset.current = null;\n        latest.current.onAnchor(null, self);\n      },\n      consumedLastMouseUp: () => consumed.current,\n    };\n    return self;\n  }, []);\n\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    /**\n     * Popover position, in the pane's content coordinates. The pane scrolls and\n     * the popover is positioned inside it, so the scroll offset is added back:\n     * client rects are measured against the viewport, not the scrolled content.\n     */\n    const anchorFor = (rect: DOMRect, mode: RcSelectionAnchor['mode']): RcSelectionAnchor => {\n      const pane = latest.current.paneRef.current!;\n      const paneRect = pane.getBoundingClientRect();\n      return {\n        x: rect.left - paneRect.left + pane.scrollLeft,\n        y: rect.bottom - paneRect.top + 6 + pane.scrollTop,\n        mode,\n      };\n    };\n\n    const onMouseUp = (event: MouseEvent) => {\n      const { textRef, highlightMode, color, onAdd, onRemove, onRemoveRange, onAnchor } =\n        latest.current;\n      const el = textRef.current;\n      if (!el) return;\n\n      const target = event.target as HTMLElement | null;\n      // A release on the popover's own buttons is the click that is about to\n      // fire. interface Popover {
  anchor: RcSelectionAnchor;

  surface: RcMarkSurfaceHandle;
}

export function RcQuestionPane({
  const paneRef = React.useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const stemRef = React.useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  // One popover for the pane. Its state is this pane's own: the passage pane
  // keeps its own, so a selection here never moves a popover there.
  const [popover, setPopover] = React.useState<Popover | null>(null);
  const onAnchor = React.useCallback(
    (anchor: RcSelectionAnchor | null, surface: RcMarkSurfaceHandle) =>
      setPopover(anchor ? { anchor, surface } : null),
    [],
  );

  const stemTarget = questionTarget(question.questionId, 'stem');
  const stemHighlights = React.useMemo(
    () => highlights.filter((h) => h.target === stemTarget),
    [highlights, stemTarget],
  );
  React.useLayoutEffect(() => {
    const el = stemRef.current;
    if (el) paintRcAnnotations(el, stemHighlights);
  }, [question.questionId, question.prompt, stemHighlights]);

  const stem = useRcMarkSurface({
    textRef: stemRef,
    paneRef,
    highlightMode,
    color,
    onAdd: (start, end, chosen, kind) => onAddHighlight(stemTarget, start, end, chosen, kind),
    onRemove: (offset) => onRemoveHighlight(stemTarget, offset),
    onRemoveRange: (start, end) => onRemoveRange(stemTarget, start, end),
    onAnchor,
  });

  /** A release anywhere else in the pane puts the popover away. */
  const dismissOutsideSurfaces = (event: React.MouseEvent) => {
    const el = event.target as HTMLElement;
    if (el.closest('[data-rc-mark-surface], .rc-selection-popover')) return;
    setPopover(null);
  };

  React.useEffect(() => {
    if (!revealed) {
      setOpenLetters(new Set());

  return (
    <div
      className="rc-exam-col relative min-w-0 overflow-y-auto px-6 py-7 sm:pl-10 sm:pr-8"
      style={type.question}
      ref={paneRef}
      onMouseUp={dismissOutsideSurfaces}
    >
      {reattemptMode && (
        <div className="rc-reattempt-banner">
          <span>
             re-attempt was answering itself. */
          const isPicked = reattemptMode
            ? reattemptPick === choice.letter
            : (reattemptPick ?? selected) === choice.letter;
          return (
            <RcChoiceRow
              key={choice.letter}
              choice={choice}
              questionId={question.questionId}
              selected={isPicked}
              eliminated={
                // Hidden outright while they answer again, and never drawn on
                // the choice they have just picked: a row that is selected AND
                // struck through reads as "you crossed out your own answer",
                // when what happened is that they landed on a choice they had
                // eliminated the first time round.
                !reattemptMode && !isPicked && eliminations.includes(choice.letter)
              }
              revealed={showFeedback}
              disabled={showFeedback}
              eliminationDisabled={reattemptMode}
              onSelect={onSelect}
              onToggleElimination={onToggleElimination}
              textStyle={type.choiceText}
              badgeStyle={type.choiceBadge}
              highlights={highlights}
              color={color}
              highlightMode={highlightMode}
              onAddHighlight={onAddHighlight}
              onRemoveHighlight={onRemoveHighlight}
              onRemoveRange={onRemoveRange}
              onAnchor={onAnchor}
              paneRef={paneRef}
            >
              {showFeedback && (
                <RcChoiceDisclosure
                  choice={choice}
                  isPicked={isPicked}
                  open={openLetters.has(choice.letter)}
                  onToggle={() =>
                    setOpenLetters((prev) => {
                      const next = new Set(prev);
                      if (next.has(choice.letter)) next.delete(choice.letter);
                      else next.add(choice.letter);
                      return next;
                    })
                  }
                />
              )}
            </RcChoiceRow>
          );
        })}
      </ul>

      <RcSelectionPopover
        anchor={popover?.anchor ?? null}
        color={color}
        onColorChange={onColorChange}
        onApply={(chosen) => popover?.surface.applyPending(chosen)}
        onRemove={() => popover?.surface.removePending()}
        onDismiss={() => setPopover(null)}
      />
    </div>
  );
}

import * as React from 'react';
import { cleanup, fireEvent, render, screen } from '@testing-library/react';
import { afterEach, beforeAll, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { RcChoiceRow } from './RcChoiceRow';
import type { RcChoice, RcHighlight, RcHighlightMode } from '../lib/rcTypes';

const choice: RcChoice = {
  letter: 'B',
  text: 'The author regards the theory as incomplete.',
  tag: null,
  percent: null,
  responseCount: null,
  correct: false,
  explanation: null,
};

interface Overrides {
  highlightMode?: RcHighlightMode;
  eliminated?: boolean;
  disabled?: boolean;
  revealed?: boolean;
  highlights?: RcHighlight[];
}

function renderRow(overrides: Overrides = {}) {
  const onSelect = vi.fn();
  const onAnchor = vi.fn();
  const paneRef = React.createRef<HTMLUListElement>();
  render(
    <ul ref={paneRef}>
      <RcChoiceRow
        choice={choice}
        questionId={42}
        selected={false}
        eliminated={overrides.eliminated ?? false}
        revealed={overrides.revealed ?? false}
        disabled={overrides.disabled}
        onSelect={onSelect}
        onToggleElimination={vi.fn()}
        highlights={overrides.highlights ?? []}
        color="yellow"
        highlightMode={overrides.highlightMode ?? 'none'}
        onAddHighlight={vi.fn()}
        onRemoveHighlight={vi.fn()}
        onRemoveRange={vi.fn()}
        onAnchor={onAnchor}
        paneRef={paneRef}
      />
    </ul>,
  );
  const text = screen.getByText(/regards the theory/);
  return { onSelect, onAnchor, text };
}

/** A drag across the choice text, as the browser leaves it behind on click. */
function selectAll(el: HTMLElement) {
  const range = document.createRange();
  range.selectNodeContents(el);
  const selection = window.getSelection()!;
  selection.removeAllRanges();

## feat/section-directions-card

## assistant

Done. Highlighting and underlining now work on the question stem and every answer choice, in every place a student drills, and it's pushed to PR #121 (now 8 commits).

## What was actually wrong

Three things, not one:

1. **Choices were `<button>`s.** A browser won't start a drag-selection inside a button, so the marker could never touch them. On every LR surface.
2. **A hidden bug was killing stem highlights.** Overlap between highlights was tested by character offset with no section check, so highlighting the opening of the stimulus silently deleted a stem highlight at the same offsets. That's why the stem felt broken even though it was wired.
3. **RC had no stem or choice surface at all**, and its store couldn't say which question a mark belonged to.

## What shipped

**LR** (drill, blind review, section walk, bootcamp capstone modal, all through one shared hook so they behave identically):
- Choice text is a plain block: a click still picks the answer, a drag never does, the letter badge stays the keyboard control.
- Each section (stimulus, stem, choice A..E) has its own lane, so marks never fight each other.
- One drag from the stem into two choices marks all three. A drag that starts on the question number snaps to the stem instead of shifting by four characters.
- Blind review and the capstone modal had the tools but dead handlers; both work now.

**RC:** stem and each choice take highlight/underline/erase with the same tools as the passage, and marks persist (scoped as `q<id>:stem` / `q<id>:A`, no migration needed).

## Review pass

I ran an adversarial review over the diff (5 lenses, every finding independently verified). Nine were real; all fixed and re-verified in the browser:
- Under a marker tool, **double-clicking a word in a choice also picked it** (first click lands before the word is selected). A pick from the text now waits 300ms while a marker is on; immediate with no tool.
- RC dropped a mark if the mouse was released in the row padding; the RC Highlight popover survived Next and could write old offsets onto the new question; the capstone modal's Directions dialog opened under the modal and Escape closed the modal from the Find box.

Five findings were refuted as pre-existing (e.g. multiply-blend marks invisible on the black/navy RC colour schemes, which was already true for passage marks).

## Verified live on the dev build

LR section, LR blind review, and RC drill: stimulus + stem + choice marks coexisting, plain click picks, drag doesn't, double-click marks only, eraser click/drag, release-outside, cross-block drag, popover reset on navigation, RC marks surviving reload.

**Tests:** 410 pass; 4 fail in `SingleSession.test.tsx` (booking slot "1:00 PM"), which fail identically on the main checkout on a different branch, so pre-existing and clock-dependent. Typecheck clean.

## Skipped, and why

- **Classic layout** (`?layout=classic`, not reachable by students without the URL param): still stimulus + stem only.
- **Causation Station** bootcamp cards use their own one-colour model; not touched.
- **LRQuestionModal** (bootcamp capstone) was verified by tests and the reviewers, not by me in the browser; I didn't have a capstone open.

Same standing questions as before: merge + publish #121, and Codex review first or not. Avneet's PT156.S2 assignment (due 2026-09-12) is still waiting on that.

      1 53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76
---
name: lovable-frontend-publish-is-separate
description: A push builds a Lovable preview but never reaches lsatprep.study; publish with deploy_project, wait for the sync, and prove it from the bundle.
metadata:
  type: project
---

Pushing to `main` makes Lovable build the commit — its project record shows a
preview screenshot tagged with the commit sha within minutes — but the published
site at lsatprep.study keeps serving the previous bundle until something calls
`deploy_project` (Lovable MCP, `project_id 53b9a6f2-aba7-4ae4-8dd1-fa5b8aab4d76`,
name `lsatu`). Confirmed 2026-09-08: a fix sat built-but-unpublished for 20
minutes with no error anywhere.

**The sync lags the push.** `deploy_project` publishes whatever Lovable has
ingested, not what is on origin. On 2026-09-09 a deploy fired seconds after a
successful push and shipped the *previous* commit; `get_project` showed
`latest_commit_sha` catching up about three minutes later, and a second deploy
was needed. There is no error and no warning — the deploy reports `pending` and
succeeds, just on the wrong commit.

**Why:** build, sync and publish are three separate steps, and none of them
reports that the others have not happened. A green push reads as shipped.

**How to apply:** before calling `deploy_project`, call `get_project` and check
`latest_commit_sha` equals your HEAD; if it does not, wait and re-check. After
deploying, prove it from the bundle: pick a string **the commit alone**
introduces — not a class name an earlier deploy already shipped, which is how a
stale bundle passed a check on 2026-09-09 — then fetch `https://lsatprep.study/`,
read the `index-*` asset hash out of it, and grep the asset for the marker.
Allow several minutes; the hash changing is the signal a publish landed at all.
Note `deploy_project` ships every unpublished commit on main, not just yours.

Same trap, different half: [[lovable-edge-functions-do-not-deploy-from-push]].
Related: [[push-only-your-own-commits]].

### 1. Section directions (the LawHub-style opening)
- Opening an assigned PT section lands on the directions page: Section N, Total Section Time, question count, official directions, GO TO QUESTIONS. Clock starts on GO TO QUESTIONS, not before. Back to home link, no floating help button.
- Inside the section, the **Directions** button swaps the page to the directions card with the clock still running top-right and a **Questions** button top-left. Verified live on dev: clock ran continuously through three round trips.
- The card is one shared component for both, with the big bold "Section 2" title.

### 2. Pause and section behaviour
- LawHub "Notice" pause dialog on every full section and PT sitting, with Cancel / Ok / "Don't show me this message again" (per sitting).
- Assigned sections are sealed by default (no Answer/Explanation mid-section), "Time's up." → Go to blind review, blind review auto-opens after an assigned section, completion self-closes.

- **RC:** stem and each choice take the same tools as the passage, persist across reload, marks land even if the mouse is released in the row padding, and the Highlight popover no longer survives into the next question.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
